`Source/Subregions.cpp`:

```cpp
/*
____________________________________________________________________________________________________
| _______  _____  __   _ _______ _______ _______                                                   |
| |  |  | |     | | \  | |______    |    |_____|                                                   |
| |  |  | |_____| |  \_| |______    |    |     |                                                   |
|__________________________________________________________________________________________________|
| Moneta ~ Usermode memory scanner & malware hunter                                                |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| https://www.forrest-orr.net/post/masking-malicious-memory-artifacts-part-ii-insights-from-moneta |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Author: Forrest Orr - 2020                                                                       |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Contact: forrest.orr@protonmail.com                                                              |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Licensed under GNU GPLv3                                                                         |
|__________________________________________________________________________________________________|
| ## Features                                                                                      |
|                                                                                                  |
| ~ Query the memory attributes of any accessible process(es).                                     |
| ~ Identify private, mapped and image memory.                                                     |
| ~ Correlate regions of memory to their underlying file on disks.                                 |
| ~ Identify PE headers and sections corresponding to image memory.                                |
| ~ Identify modified regions of mapped image memory.                                              |
| ~ Identify abnormal memory attributes indicative of malware.                                     |
| ~ Create memory dumps of user-specified memory ranges                                            |
| ~ Calculate memory permission/type statistics                                                    |
|__________________________________________________________________________________________________|

*/

#include "StdAfx.h"
#include "Memory.hpp"
#include "Interface.hpp"
#include "Processes.hpp"

using namespace std;
using namespace Memory;
using namespace Processes;

Subregion::Subregion(Processes::Process &OwnerProc, const MEMORY_BASIC_INFORMATION* Mbi) : Basic(Mbi), PrivateSize(0), ProcessHandle(OwnerProc.GetHandle()), Flags(0) {
	vector<Processes::Thread*> Threads = OwnerProc.GetThreads();
	vector<void*> Heaps = OwnerProc.GetHeaps();

	for (vector<Processes::Thread*>::const_iterator ThItr = Threads.begin(); ThItr != Threads.end(); ++ThItr) {
		if ((*ThItr)->GetEntryPoint() >= this->Basic->BaseAddress && (*ThItr)->GetEntryPoint() < (static_cast<uint8_t *>(this->Basic->BaseAddress) + this->Basic->RegionSize)) {
			this->Threads.push_back(new Processes::Thread((*ThItr)->GetTid(), OwnerProc));
		}

		if ((*ThItr)->GetStackAddress() >= this->Basic->BaseAddress && (*ThItr)->GetStackAddress() < (static_cast<uint8_t*>(this->Basic->BaseAddress) + this->Basic->RegionSize)) {
			this->Flags |= MEMORY_SUBREGION_FLAG_STACK;
		}

		if ((*ThItr)->GetTebAddress() >= this->Basic->BaseAddress && (*ThItr)->GetTebAddress() < (static_cast<uint8_t*>(this->Basic->BaseAddress) + this->Basic->RegionSize)) {
			this->Flags |= MEMORY_SUBREGION_FLAG_TEB;
		}
	}

	if (OwnerProc.GetImageBase() >= this->Basic->BaseAddress && OwnerProc.GetImageBase() < (static_cast<uint8_t*>(this->Basic->BaseAddress) + this->Basic->RegionSize)) {
		this->Flags |= MEMORY_SUBREGION_FLAG_BASE_IMAGE;
	}

	if (find(Heaps.begin(), Heaps.end(), Mbi->BaseAddress) != Heaps.end()) {
		this->Flags |= MEMORY_SUBREGION_FLAG_HEAP;
	}

	if (Mbi->State == MEM_COMMIT && Mbi->Type != MEM_PRIVATE) {
		this->PrivateSize = this->QueryPrivateSize(); // Querying the working set is one of the greatest performance drains in the tool and should be done sparingly
	}
}

Subregion::~Subregion() {
	if (this->Basic != nullptr) {
		delete Basic;
	}

	for (vector<Processes::Thread*>::const_iterator Itr = this->Threads.begin(); Itr != this->Threads.end(); ++Itr) {
		delete* Itr;
	}
}

const wchar_t* Subregion::ProtectSymbol(uint32_t dwProtect) {
	switch (dwProtect) {
		case PAGE_READONLY: return L"R";
		case PAGE_READWRITE: return L"RW";
		case PAGE_EXECUTE_READ: return L"RX";
		case PAGE_EXECUTE_READWRITE: return L"RWX";
		case PAGE_EXECUTE_WRITECOPY: return L"RWXC";
		case PAGE_EXECUTE: return L"X";
		case PAGE_WRITECOPY: return L"WC";
		case PAGE_NOACCESS: return L"NA";
		case PAGE_WRITECOMBINE: return L"WCB";
		case (PAGE_GUARD | PAGE_READWRITE): // Typically these flags are never combined: page guard is an exception
		case PAGE_GUARD: return L"PG";
		case PAGE_NOCACHE: return L"NC";
		case 0: return L"-";
		default:  return L"?";
	}
}

const wchar_t* Subregion::StateSymbol(uint32_t dwState) {
	switch (dwState) {
		case MEM_COMMIT: return L"Commit";
		case MEM_FREE: return L"Free";
		case MEM_RESERVE: return L"Reserve";
		default: return L"?";
	}
}

const wchar_t* Subregion::AttribDesc(const MEMORY_BASIC_INFORMATION* Mbi) {
	switch (Mbi->State) {
		case MEM_COMMIT: return ProtectSymbol(Mbi->Protect);
		case MEM_FREE: return L"Free";
		case MEM_RESERVE: return L"Reserve";
		default: return L"?";
	}
}

const wchar_t* Subregion::TypeSymbol(uint32_t dwType) {
	switch (dwType) {
		case MEM_IMAGE: return L"IMG";
		case MEM_MAPPED: return L"MAP";
		case MEM_PRIVATE: return L"PRV";
		default: return L"?";
	}
}

uint32_t Subregion::QueryPrivateSize() const {
	static RtlGetVersion_t RtlGetVersion = reinterpret_cast<RtlGetVersion_t>(GetProcAddress(GetModuleHandleW(L"ntdll.dll"), "RtlGetVersion"));
	static RTL_OSVERSIONINFOW osvi = { 0 };
	uint32_t dwPrivateSize = 0;

	if (RtlGetVersion && !osvi.dwBuildNumber) {
		osvi.dwOSVersionInfoSize = sizeof(osvi);
		RtlGetVersion(&osvi);
	}

	if (this->Basic->State == MEM_COMMIT && this->Basic->Protect != PAGE_NOACCESS && this->Basic->Type == MEM_IMAGE) { // Optimize performance by skipping working set scan for non-image memory, as this data is not valuable for private and mapped types.
		MEMORY_WORKING_SET_EX_INFORMATION WorkingSets = { 0 };

		for (uint32_t dwPageOffset = 0; dwPageOffset < this->Basic->RegionSize; dwPageOffset += 0x1000) {
			WorkingSets.VirtualAddress = (static_cast<uint8_t *>(this->Basic->BaseAddress) + dwPageOffset);
			if (K32QueryWorkingSetEx(this->ProcessHandle, &WorkingSets, sizeof(MEMORY_WORKING_SET_EX_INFORMATION))) {
				// Use SharedOriginal after RS3/1709
				// https://windows-internals.com/understanding-a-new-mitigation-module-tampering-protection/
				if (osvi.dwBuildNumber >= 16299) {
					if (!WorkingSets.VirtualAttributes.SharedOriginal) {
						dwPrivateSize += 0x1000;
					}
				} else {
					if (!WorkingSets.VirtualAttributes.Shared) {
						dwPrivateSize += 0x1000;
					}
				}
			}
			else {
				Interface::Log(Interface::VerbosityLevel::Debug, "... failed to query working set at 0x%p\r\n", WorkingSets.VirtualAddress);
			}
		}
	}

	return dwPrivateSize;
}

bool Subregion::PageExecutable(uint32_t dwProtect) {
	return (dwProtect == PAGE_EXECUTE || dwProtect == PAGE_EXECUTE_READ || dwProtect == PAGE_EXECUTE_READWRITE);
}
```

`Source/Thread.cpp`:

```cpp
/*
____________________________________________________________________________________________________
| _______  _____  __   _ _______ _______ _______                                                   |
| |  |  | |     | | \  | |______    |    |_____|                                                   |
| |  |  | |_____| |  \_| |______    |    |     |                                                   |
|__________________________________________________________________________________________________|
| Moneta ~ Usermode memory scanner & malware hunter                                                |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| https://www.forrest-orr.net/post/masking-malicious-memory-artifacts-part-ii-insights-from-moneta |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Author: Forrest Orr - 2020                                                                       |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Contact: forrest.orr@protonmail.com                                                              |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Licensed under GNU GPLv3                                                                         |
|__________________________________________________________________________________________________|
| ## Features                                                                                      |
|                                                                                                  |
| ~ Query the memory attributes of any accessible process(es).                                     |
| ~ Identify private, mapped and image memory.                                                     |
| ~ Correlate regions of memory to their underlying file on disks.                                 |
| ~ Identify PE headers and sections corresponding to image memory.                                |
| ~ Identify modified regions of mapped image memory.                                              |
| ~ Identify abnormal memory attributes indicative of malware.                                     |
| ~ Create memory dumps of user-specified memory ranges                                            |
| ~ Calculate memory permission/type statistics                                                    |
|__________________________________________________________________________________________________|

*/

#include "StdAfx.h"
#include "Interface.hpp"
#include "Processes.hpp"
#include "PEB.h"

using namespace std;
using namespace Processes;

Thread::~Thread() {
	CloseHandle(this->Handle);
}

Thread::Thread(uint32_t dwTid, Processes::Process &OwnerProc) : Id(dwTid), Handle(nullptr), StartAddress(nullptr), TebAddress(nullptr), StackAddress(nullptr) {
	this->Handle = OpenThread(THREAD_QUERY_INFORMATION | THREAD_GET_CONTEXT, false, this->Id); // OpenThreadToken consistently failed even with impersonation (ERROR_NO_TOKEN). The idea was abandoned due to lack of relevance. Get-InjectedThread returns the user as SYSTEM even when it was a regular user which launched the remote thread.

	if (this->Handle == nullptr) { // Threads come and go while the snapshot is being walked: skip a vanished thread instead of aborting the whole scan.
		Interface::Log(Interface::VerbosityLevel::Debug, "... failed to open thread %d (error %d): skipping thread metadata\r\n", this->Id, GetLastError());
		return;
	}

	static NtQueryInformationThread_t NtQueryInformationThread = reinterpret_cast<NtQueryInformationThread_t>(GetProcAddress(GetModuleHandleW(L"ntdll.dll"), "NtQueryInformationThread"));
	void* pStartAddress = nullptr;
	HANDLE hDupThread = nullptr;

	if (!DuplicateHandle(GetCurrentProcess(), this->Handle, GetCurrentProcess(), &hDupThread, THREAD_QUERY_INFORMATION, FALSE, 0)) { // Without duplicating this handle NtQueryInformationThread will consistently fail to query the start address.
		Interface::Log(Interface::VerbosityLevel::Debug, "... failed to duplicate thread %d handle (error %d): skipping thread metadata\r\n", this->Id, GetLastError());
		return;
	}

	NTSTATUS NtStatus = NtQueryInformationThread(hDupThread, static_cast<THREADINFOCLASS>(ThreadQuerySetWin32StartAddress), &pStartAddress, sizeof(pStartAddress), nullptr);
	THREAD_BASIC_INFORMATION Tbi = { 0 };

	if (NT_SUCCESS(NtStatus)) {
		this->StartAddress = pStartAddress;
	}

	NtStatus = NtQueryInformationThread(hDupThread, static_cast<THREADINFOCLASS>(ThreadBasicInformation), &Tbi, sizeof(THREAD_BASIC_INFORMATION), nullptr);

	if (NT_SUCCESS(NtStatus)) {
		Interface::Log(Interface::VerbosityLevel::Debug, "... TEB of 0x%p\r\n", Tbi.TebBaseAddress);
		this->TebAddress = Tbi.TebBaseAddress;

		if (OwnerProc.IsWow64()) {
			unique_ptr<TEB32> LocalTeb = make_unique<TEB32>();
			uint32_t dwTebSize = sizeof(TEB32);

			if (ReadProcessMemory(OwnerProc.GetHandle(), Tbi.TebBaseAddress, LocalTeb.get(), dwTebSize, nullptr)) {
				Interface::Log(Interface::VerbosityLevel::Debug, "... successfully read remote TEB to local memory.\r\n");
				Interface::Log(Interface::VerbosityLevel::Debug, "... stack base: 0x%08x\r\n", LocalTeb->StackBase);
				this->StackAddress = reinterpret_cast<void*>(LocalTeb->StackBase);
			}
			else { // A thread can exit between the snapshot and here. Losing its stack metadata must not invalidate the whole scan.
				Interface::Log(Interface::VerbosityLevel::Debug, "... failed to read remote TEB of thread %d (error %d): thread metadata unavailable\r\n", this->Id, GetLastError());
			}
		}
		else {
			unique_ptr<TEB64> LocalTeb = make_unique<TEB64>();
			uint32_t dwTebSize = sizeof(TEB64);

			if (ReadProcessMemory(OwnerProc.GetHandle(), Tbi.TebBaseAddress, LocalTeb.get(), dwTebSize, nullptr)) {
				Interface::Log(Interface::VerbosityLevel::Debug, "... successfully read remote TEB to local memory.\r\n");
				Interface::Log(Interface::VerbosityLevel::Debug, "... stack base: 0x%p\r\n", LocalTeb->StackBase);
				this->StackAddress = LocalTeb->StackBase;
			}
			else {
				Interface::Log(Interface::VerbosityLevel::Debug, "... failed to read remote TEB of thread %d (error %d): thread metadata unavailable\r\n", this->Id, GetLastError());
			}
		}
	}
	else {
		Interface::Log(Interface::VerbosityLevel::Debug, "... failed to query TEB of thread %d (NTSTATUS 0x%08x): thread metadata unavailable\r\n", this->Id, NtStatus);
	}

	CloseHandle(hDupThread);
}

```

<root>
  <!-- 
    Microsoft ResX Schema 
    
    Version 2.0
    
    The primary goals of this format is to allow a simple XML format 
    that is mostly human readable. The generation and parsing of the 
    various data types are done through the TypeConverter classes 
    associated with the data types.
    
    Example:
    
    ... ado.net/XML headers & schema ...
    <resheader name="resmimetype">text/microsoft-resx</resheader>
    <resheader name="version">2.0</resheader>
    <resheader name="reader">System.Resources.ResXResourceReader, System.Windows.Forms, ...</resheader>
    <resheader name="writer">System.Resources.ResXResourceWriter, System.Windows.Forms, ...</resheader>
    <data name="Name1"><value>this is my long string</value><comment>this is a comment</comment></data>
    <data name="Color1" type="System.Drawing.Color, System.Drawing">Blue</data>
    <data name="Bitmap1" mimetype="application/x-microsoft.net.object.binary.base64">
        <value>[base64 mime encoded serialized .NET Framework object]</value>
    </data>
    <data name="Icon1" type="System.Drawing.Icon, System.Drawing" mimetype="application/x-microsoft.net.object.bytearray.base64">
        <value>[base64 mime encoded string representing a byte array form of the .NET Framework object]</value>
        <comment>This is a comment</comment>
    </data>
                
    There are any number of "resheader" rows that contain simple 
    name/value pairs.
    
    Each data row contains a name, and value. The row also contains a 
    type or mimetype. Type corresponds to a .NET class that support 
    text/value conversion through the TypeConverter architecture. 
    Classes that don't support this are serialized and stored with the 
    mimetype set.
    
    The mimetype is used for serialized objects, and tells the 
    ResXResourceReader how to depersist the object. This is currently not 
    extensible. For a given mimetype the value must be set accordingly:
    
    Note - application/x-microsoft.net.object.binary.base64 is the format 
    that the ResXResourceWriter will generate, however the reader can 
    read any of the formats listed below.
    
    mimetype: application/x-microsoft.net.object.binary.base64
    value   : The object must be serialized with 
            : System.Runtime.Serialization.Formatters.Binary.BinaryFormatter
            : and then encoded with base64 encoding.
    
    mimetype: application/x-microsoft.net.object.soap.base64
    value   : The object must be serialized with 
            : System.Runtime.Serialization.Formatters.Soap.SoapFormatter
            : and then encoded with base64 encoding.

    mimetype: application/x-microsoft.net.object.bytearray.base64
    value   : The object must be serialized into a byte array 
            : using a System.ComponentModel.TypeConverter
            : and then encoded with base64 encoding.
    -->
  <xsd:schema id="root" xmlns="" xmlns:xsd="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema" xmlns:msdata="urn:schemas-microsoft-com:xml-msdata">
    <xsd:import namespace="http://www.w3.org/XML/1998/namespace" />
    <xsd:element name="root" msdata:IsDataSet="true">
      <xsd:complexType>
        <xsd:choice maxOccurs="unbounded">
          <xsd:element name="metadata">
            <xsd:complexType>
              <xsd:sequence>
                <xsd:element name="value" type="xsd:string" minOccurs="0" />
              </xsd:sequence>
              <xsd:attribute name="name" use="required" type="xsd:string" />
              <xsd:attribute name="type" type="xsd:string" />
              <xsd:attribute name="mimetype" type="xsd:string" />
              <xsd:attribute ref="xml:space" />
            </xsd:complexType>
          </xsd:element>
          <xsd:element name="assembly">
            <xsd:complexType>
              <xsd:attribute name="alias" type="xsd:string" />
              <xsd:attribute name="name" type="xsd:string" />
            </xsd:complexType>
          </xsd:element>
          <xsd:element name="data">
            <xsd:complexType>
              <xsd:sequence>
                <xsd:element name="value" type="xsd:string" minOccurs="0" msdata:Ordinal="1" />
                <xsd:element name="comment" type="xsd:string" minOccurs="0" msdata:Ordinal="2" />
              </xsd:sequence>
              <xsd:attribute name="name" type="xsd:string" use="required" msdata:Ordinal="1" />

          <xsd:element name="resheader">
            <xsd:complexType>
              <xsd:sequence>
                <xsd:element name="value" type="xsd:string" minOccurs="0" msdata:Ordinal="1" />
              </xsd:sequence>
              <xsd:attribute name="name" type="xsd:string" use="required" />
            </xsd:complexType>
          </xsd:element>
        </xsd:choice>
      </xsd:complexType>
    </xsd:element>
  </xsd:schema>
  <resheader name="resmimetype">
    <value>text/microsoft-resx</value>
  </resheader>
  <resheader name="version">
    <value>2.0</value>

</root>

`Tests/DotNetFrameworkExe/Program.cs`:

```cs
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Windows.Forms;
using System.Diagnostics;
using System.ComponentModel;

namespace DotNetFrameworkExe
{
    static class Program
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// The main entry point for the application.
        /// </summary>
        [STAThread]
        static void Main()
        {
            Process currentProcess = Process.GetCurrentProcess();
            Console.Out.WriteLine("Current PID: " + currentProcess.Id);
            Application.EnableVisualStyles();
            Application.SetCompatibleTextRenderingDefault(false);
            Application.Run(new Form1());
            var result = MessageBox.Show("test", ".NET framework EXE",
                     MessageBoxButtons.YesNo,
                     MessageBoxIcon.Question);
        }
    }
}

```

    </data>
    <data name="Icon1" type="System.Drawing.Icon, System.Drawing" mimetype="application/x-microsoft.net.object.bytearray.base64">
        <value>[base64 mime encoded string representing a byte array form of the .NET Framework object]</value>
        <comment>This is a comment</comment>
    </data>
                
    There are any number of "resheader" rows that contain simple 
    name/value pairs.
    
    Each data row contains a name, and value. The row also contains a 
    type or mimetype. Type corresponds to a .NET class that support 
    text/value conversion through the TypeConverter architecture. 
    Classes that don't support this are serialized and stored with the 
    mimetype set.
    
    The mimetype is used for serialized objects, and tells the 
    ResXResourceReader how to depersist the object. This is currently not 
    extensible. For a given mimetype the value must be set accordingly:
    
    Note - application/x-microsoft.net.object.binary.base64 is the format 
    that the ResXResourceWriter will generate, however the reader can 
    read any of the formats listed below.
    
    mimetype: application/x-microsoft.net.object.binary.base64
    value   : The object must be serialized with 
            : System.Serialization.Formatters.Binary.BinaryFormatter
            : and then encoded with base64 encoding.
    
    mimetype: application/x-microsoft.net.object.soap.base64
    value   : The object must be serialized with 
            : System.Runtime.Serialization.Formatters.Soap.SoapFormatter
            : and then encoded with base64 encoding.

    mimetype: application/x-microsoft.net.object.bytearray.base64
    value   : The object must be serialized into a byte array 
            : using a System.ComponentModel.TypeConverter
            : and then encoded with base64 encoding.
    -->
  <xsd:schema id="root" xmlns="" xmlns:xsd="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema" xmlns:msdata="urn:schemas-microsoft-com:xml-msdata">
    <xsd:element name="root" msdata:IsDataSet="true">
      <xsd:complexType>
        <xsd:choice maxOccurs="unbounded">
          <xsd:element name="metadata">
            <xsd:complexType>
              <xsd:sequence>
                <xsd:element name="value" type="xsd:string" minOccurs="0" />
              </xsd:sequence>
              <xsd:attribute name="name" type="xsd:string" />
              <xsd:attribute name="type" type="xsd:string" />
              <xsd:attribute name="mimetype" type="xsd:string" />
            </xsd:complexType>
          </xsd:element>
          <xsd:element name="assembly">
            <xsd:complexType>
              <xsd:attribute name="alias" type="xsd:string" />
              <xsd:attribute name="name" type="xsd:string" />
            </xsd:complexType>
          </xsd:element>
          <xsd:element name="data">
            <xsd:complexType>
              <xsd:sequence>
                <xsd:element name="value" type="xsd:string" minOccurs="0" msdata:Ordinal="1" />
                <xsd:element name="comment" type="xsd:string" minOccurs="0" msdata:Ordinal="2" />
              </xsd:sequence>
              <xsd:attribute name="name" type="xsd:string" msdata:Ordinal="1" />
              <xsd:attribute name="type" type="xsd:string" msdata:Ordinal="3" />
              <xsd:attribute name="mimetype" type="xsd:string" msdata:Ordinal="4" />
            </xsd:complexType>
          </xsd:element>
          <xsd:element name="resheader">
            <xsd:complexType>
              <xsd:sequence>
                <xsd:element name="value" type="xsd:string" minOccurs="0" msdata:Ordinal="1" />
              </xsd:sequence>
              <xsd:attribute name="name" type="xsd:string" use="required" />
            </xsd:complexType>
          </xsd:element>
        </xsd:choice>
      </xsd:complexType>
    </xsd:element>
  </xsd:schema>
  <resheader name="resmimetype">
    <value>text/microsoft-resx</value>
  </resheader>
  <resheader name="version">
    <value>2.0</value>
  </resheader>
  <resheader name="reader">
    <value>System.Resources.ResXResourceReader, System.Windows.Forms, Version=2.0.0.0, Culture=neutral, PublicKeyToken=b77a5c561934e089</value>
  </resheader>
  <resheader name="writer">
    <value>System.Resources.ResXResourceWriter, System.Windows.Forms, Version=2.0.0.0, Culture=neutral, PublicKeyToken=b77a5c561934e089</value>
  </resheader>
</root>

`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`:

```md
# Architecture

## Why a GUI around Moneta

Moneta is a console scanner: you give it a PID, a memory selection and a verbosity, and it
prints findings. HYPERSENSEGLOBAL keeps that engine verbatim and adds the parts an analyst
actually wants during triage — a process picker, mode switches, filters, a live stream and
a report you can hand to someone else.

## Process model

```
┌──────────────────────────────┐
│ HyperSense64.exe             │
│  GUI thread  (Win32, D2D)    │
│  reader thread ── pipe ──┐   │
└──────────────────────────┼───┘
                           │  CreateProcessW(self, "--engine …", CREATE_NO_WINDOW)
                           ▼
                 ┌─────────────────────────┐
                 │ HyperSense64.exe        │
                 │  --engine  (Moneta)     │
                 │  SeDebugPrivilege ↑     │
                 └─────────────────────────┘
```

`WinMain` inspects its own command line with `CommandLineToArgvW`. When the first argument is
`--engine`, it calls `MonetaMain(argc - 1, argv + 1)` and exits; otherwise it starts the GUI.

The GUI spawns **itself** with `--engine` and the Moneta equivalent of the current UI
settings. Consequences:

* **Fault isolation.** The scanner touches third-party memory for a living. If it faults,
  the exit code lands in the log and the window stays alive — no lost session.
* **Clean cancellation.** `STOP` calls `TerminateProcess` on the child; there is no global
  state to unwind and no half-freed engine heap to reason about.
* **No second binary.** Shipping is one file. The equivalent command line is echoed into the
  live view and stored in the report, so a run is reproducible from a terminal.

The child is created with an inherited write handle on an anonymous pipe, `CREATE_NO_WINDOW`
and `STARTF_USESTDHANDLES`; a reader thread turns the byte stream into lines and posts them
to the UI. Mapping UI state to engine arguments is a pure function, which keeps the CLI and
the GUI in agreement.

## Source layout

| Path | Contents |
| --- | --- |
| `HyperSense/src/main.cpp` | Entire front end: window, custom controls, theme, process enumeration and search, argument mapping, child process handling, report writers. |
| `HyperSense/build.bat` | Single-command build (MinGW-w64 + windres). |
| `HyperSense/res/` | Icon, manifest, version resource. |
| `HyperSense/tools/` | Signing helper and PowerShell UI-automation tests used to verify releases. |
| `Source/` | Moneta engine translation units (upstream + the fixes listed in `NOTICE.md`). |
| `Headers/` | Moneta headers. |
| `Resources/`, `Tests/`, `*.vcxproj` | Upstream MSVC project files, kept for reference. |

## Engine life cycle

1. `MonetaMain` parses the argument vector (`-m`, `-p`, `-v`, `-o`, `-f` …).
2. `SeDebugPrivilege` is granted to the child only.
3. The target's PEB, module list and threads are enumerated; regions are walked with
   `VirtualQueryEx` and filtered by the selected memory mode.
4. Each region is examined for IOC patterns (private executable memory, modified image
   pages, unsigned/metadata-less modules, CLR heaps, WOW64 init stubs).
5. Statistics and hits are printed as they are found.
6. Exit code `1` means "scan finished".

The UI never interprets engine output beyond line classification (surface / info / warning /
hit) and the `[ RESULT ]` line used for the summary.

## Reports

`BuildReportText()` and `BuildReportHtml()` render the same model: host, account, SID, OS
build, elevation, engine identification, target, options, timings, exit code, the `RESULT`
row and the complete (uncapped) engine log. The HTML report is a single self-contained file
with inline CSS — no external assets, safe to email.

## Threading

* GUI thread: window procedure, painting, all control state.
* Reader thread: blocking reads from the child pipe, line splitting, `PostMessage` to the
  window.
* Scan-child lifetime is owned by the GUI; `g_scanGen` invalidates late messages from a
  previous run so a stale reader can never paint into a new session.

```

`docs/BUILDING.md`:

```md
# Building from source

## Requirements

| | |
| --- | --- |
| OS | Windows 10 or 11, x64 |
| Compiler | MinGW-w64 via [MSYS2](https://www.msys2.org/), **UCRT64** environment |
| Resources | `windres` (ships with the toolchain) |
| Optional | Python 3 + Pillow — only to regenerate the icon if `res/icon.ico` is missing |

Install the toolchain once:

```bat
winget install MSYS2.MSYS2
```

then in an MSYS2 UCRT64 shell:

```bash
pacman -S --needed mingw-w64-ucrt-x86_64-gcc
```

## Build

```bat
cd HyperSense
build.bat            :: GUI flavour      -> HyperSense64.exe
build.bat classic    :: CLASSIC flavour  -> HyperSense64Classic.exe
```

The classic flavour is the same program compiled with `-DHS_CLASSIC_DEFAULT`: it opens in the
`moneta64.exe`-style one-row layout (`READY - CLASSIC`) instead of the sidebar. Its resource is
built separately (`-DHS_CLASSIC_BUILD`) so the version block reports
`OriginalFilename = HyperSense64Classic.exe` and `FileDescription = ... (x64, Classic UI)`.

The script:

1. locates `g++` on `PATH`, or falls back to `C:\msys64\ucrt64\bin\g++.exe` /
   `C:\msys64\mingw64\bin\g++.exe`, and puts that directory on `PATH` (the compiler needs
   its own DLLs);
2. regenerates `res\icon.ico` with `tools\make_icon.py` **only** when the file is missing;
3. compiles `res\resource.rc` (icon + manifest + version info) to COFF with `windres`;
4. compiles `src\main.cpp` plus every `..\Source\*.cpp` engine translation unit as one
   static, stripped executable;
5. copies the result to the repository root.

Outputs:

```
HyperSense\HyperSense64.exe         (GUI build product)
HyperSense64.exe                    (copy, this is the file you ship)
HyperSense\HyperSense64Classic.exe  (CLASSIC build product)
HyperSense64Classic.exe             (copy)
```

## Verify the build

The engine is not a separate program, but it is reachable for testing:

```bat
:: run a scan of an existing process by PID
.\HyperSense64.exe --engine -m ioc -v detail --option statistics -p 1234

:: scan everything (long)
.\HyperSense64.exe --engine -m ioc -v surface -p 1234
```

Exit codes: **1 = scan completed**, `0 = usage error`, anything else = crash. A report of
`exit 1 (scan complete)` in the UI is the expected result.

A quick smoke test that needs no target:

```bat
.\HyperSense64.exe --help
```

## Continuous integration

`.github/workflows/build.yml` builds the same executable on `windows-latest` with MSYS2 and
uploads it as a workflow artifact. Tags matching `v*` attach the binary to a draft release.

## Troubleshooting

**`g++ not found`** — install the UCRT64 compiler (above) or add
`C:\msys64\ucrt64\bin` to `PATH`.

**`cannot execute binary file` / missing `libstdc++-6.dll`** — a non-static build. The
`-static -static-libgcc -static-libstdc++` flags in `build.bat` are what make the executable
standalone; keep them.

**Build fails on a path containing `!`** — the script keeps cmd's *delayed expansion*
disabled on purpose. Do not add `setlocal EnableDelayedExpansion`.

**`0xC0000374` (heap corruption) at exit** — a `new[]` buffer is being freed with scalar
`delete`. See `NOTICE.md` §3 for the ones already fixed.

**Debugging** — MinGW has no usable `gdb` on Windows here. Use WinDbg/CDB:

```
"C:\Program Files (x86)\Windows Kits\10\Debuggers\x64\cdb.exe" ^
  -c "sxe c0000374; g; kb 60" HyperSense64.exe --engine -m ioc -v detail -p <pid>
```

Addresses are image-relative + `0x140000000`; resolve them with
`addr2line -e HyperSense64.exe -f -C <va>`.

```

`docs/FALSE_POSITIVES.md`:

```md
# Antivirus false positives (VirusTotal / Defender / SmartScreen)

`HyperSense64.exe` is a **memory scanner**. That is the whole point of the tool: it opens other
processes with `PROCESS_VM_READ`, walks their PEB/TEB, calls `VirtualQueryEx`, reads module
images and enables `SeDebugPrivilege` before a scan. Binaries that do this are routinely
scored as PUA / hacktool by machine-learning engines — signed or not.

Typical report for this tool:

| Vendor | Verdict | What it actually is |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Microsoft | **clean** on the unsigned build of this code | earlier builds were scored `Program:Win32/Wacapew.Cml` (PUA) and `Trojan:Win32/Wacatac.C!ml` / `Wacatac.Biml` for the same code |
| SecureAge | `Malicious` | generic static-ML verdict |

Everything else comes back clean (1/71 on VirusTotal for the last scanned build), and the
Moneta engine it embeds is public GPLv3 source: <https://github.com/forrest-orr/moneta>.
Version 1.0.2 ships four files — GUI and CLASSIC, each signed and unsigned — so every hash is
new and each one needs its own scan before anyone quotes a count for it.

> A 1/71 result is **not** evidence of malware, but it is worth fixing properly. Do **not**
> try to "evade" it with packers, crypters, string obfuscation or anti-VM tricks - those
> raise the ML score and get the binary classified as a hacktool for real.

> The verdict name is not a property of the code. Microsoft's cloud model re-scores the same
> bytes over time and gives different answers for different builds: this project's
> `745206FD...` build went from `2/70` (`Wacapew`) to `1/70` (clean) with no rebuild and no
> submission, and later builds came back as `Wacatac.C!ml` and then `Wacapew.Cml` again.
> Rebuilding changes the hash, not the behaviour - treat a rebuild as a lottery ticket, not a
> fix. The fix is section 2 (report) and section 1 (sign).

---

## 1. Sign the binary (biggest single fix)

An Authenticode signature binds the file to a publisher identity. SmartScreen stops treating
it as anonymous, and AV vendors can whitelist the certificate instead of the hash.
An **EV code-signing certificate** is the fastest route to SmartScreen reputation.

The 1.0.2 release binaries **are signed**: `CN=HYPERSENSEGLOBALT`, SHA-256, DigiCert RFC 3161
timestamp, `Get-AuthenticodeSignature` reports `Valid`. That certificate is **self-signed**,
which is honest but local: machines that have not installed its root still show *Unknown
publisher*, and no reputation accrues to it. Buying an OV/EV certificate from a CA and
re-signing the same bytes is the upgrade — the signature is the only thing that changes.

```powershell
# from HyperSense\
.\tools\sign.ps1 -Pfx C:\certs\hypersense.pfx -Password 'pfx-password'

# or, if the certificate is already in CurrentUser\My:
.\tools\sign.ps1 -Subject "HYPERSENSEGLOBAL"

# check an existing signature
.\tools\sign.ps1 -VerifyOnly
```

The script uses `signtool` from the Windows SDK, SHA-256 + RFC 3161 timestamping (DigiCert),
and verifies with `/pa` when done. Sign the **final** build - every rebuild changes the hash,
and signing changes it too, so hash and document the signed file, not the one before it.

## 2. Report the false positive to the vendors

Submit the **exact file/hash you ship**, once, after the final build:

```powershell
Get-FileHash .\HyperSense64.exe -Algorithm SHA256

# or let the helper print it, copy the paste text and open the portal:
.\HyperSense\tools\report_fp.ps1 -Open
```

* **Microsoft** - <https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/wdsi/filesubmission>
  Choose *Software developer* -> *Incorrectly detected as malware/malicious*, product
  *Microsoft Defender Antivirus (Windows 11/10)*, company `HYPERSENSEGLOBAL`, detection name
  `Program:Win32/Wacapew.Cml`, upload the exe and paste the text below (it fits the 1,900-char
  limit). The exact field-by-field walkthrough, with the current hash, is in
  **[MICROSOFT_FP.txt](MICROSOFT_FP.txt)**. Microsoft normally reclassifies within 24-72 h
  and pushes updated signatures.
* **SecureAge** - false-positive / support form on <https://www.secureage.com/> (include the
  same text and the SHA-256); see **[SECUREAGE_FP.txt](SECUREAGE_FP.txt)**.
* Any other vendor that flags it: same message, same hash.

Submission text:

```
False positive: HYPERSENSEGLOBAL / HyperSense64.exe

HyperSense64.exe is a usermode memory-analysis front-end that embeds the open-source
Moneta scanner (GPLv3, https://github.com/forrest-orr/moneta). Reading another process's
memory is its documented function: it uses OpenProcess(PROCESS_VM_READ), VirtualQueryEx,
PEB/TEB reads and SeDebugPrivilege to map the target's regions and report suspicious
memory (private executable pages, modified code, unsigned modules).

It is not malware: no persistence, no services, no drivers, no network traffic, no
credential access, no code injection into other processes, no files written outside the
report the user explicitly saves. The GUI runs asInvoker; elevation is opt-in.

Detection name: Program:Win32/Wacapew.Cml (ML, PUA) / SecureAge "Malicious".
Please reclassify this file.

SHA-256: <paste hash>
Engine source: https://github.com/forrest-orr/moneta
```

## 3. Keep the build "boring"

Already done in this build, and worth preserving:

* statically linked, **not** packed, no UPX, no self-modifying code, no encrypted strings;
* complete version resource (company, product, description, copyright, comments) - see
  `res/resource.rc`;
* `asInvoker` manifest (no always-on admin), no service, no driver, no autorun;
* the only network-adjacent action is none - the tool never connects anywhere;
* SeDebugPrivilege is enabled only inside the hidden scan child, right before a scan.

The import table is the part machine learning reads first, and it is deliberately dull:

* **no clipboard APIs** - `Copy selection / Copy line / Copy all output` use `WM_COPY` on the
  program's own read-only edit control, so `OpenClipboard`/`SetClipboardData` are absent;
* **no key-state polling** - Ctrl+click reads `MK_CONTROL` from the mouse message instead of
  calling `GetKeyState`/`GetAsyncKeyState`, which is the keylogger-shaped part of the profile;
* **no crypto provider** - SHA-256 (used only to build a VirusTotal URL) lives in
  `src/sha256.h`; `CryptAcquireContext`/`CryptHashData` are not imported;
* **no temp-directory writes** - the opt-in RPC trace is written next to the executable.

A build that imports `ReadProcessMemory` *and* the clipboard *and* key-state polling *and* a
hash provider looks like a Discord/credential stealer to a cloud ML model, which is what the
`Wacatac` names label. Removing the last three costs nothing functionally.

That said, it is not sufficient on its own: the same build with those imports removed still
comes back as `Wacapew`/`Wacatac` from Microsoft. The residual score comes from the tool's
purpose - a user-mode memory reader is PUA-shaped by definition - so the vendor-side steps in
sections 1 and 2 are what actually clear it.

If you add features later, avoid: process injection, `CreateRemoteThread`, keyboard hooks,
clipboard readers, `GetAsyncKeyState`, `GetTempPath`, Defender tampering, hidden windows, and
downloading payloads - each one moves the score in the wrong direction.

## 4. Users who still get flagged

Only on a machine you control: add a Defender exclusion for the folder, or restore the file
from **Windows Security -> Protection history -> Allow**. Never ask end users to disable
real-time protection - report the hash instead.

```

Text for "Additional information"
----------------------------------
Helper: `powershell -File HyperSense\tools\report_fp.ps1 -Open` recomputes the
hash, copies this text to the clipboard and opens the portal.

HyperSense64.exe is a usermode memory-analysis GUI that embeds the open-source
Moneta scanner (GPLv3, https://github.com/forrest-orr/moneta). The detection
Program:Win32/Wacapew.Cml is a machine-learning false positive: the tool is
classified as a PUA because it reads the memory of a process the user picks.

What it does, only after the user presses RUN: opens a user-selected process
with OpenProcess(PROCESS_VM_READ), calls VirtualQueryEx, reads PEB/TEB and
module images, and reports suspicious memory regions (private executable pages,
modified code, unsigned modules). SeDebugPrivilege is enabled only inside the
hidden child process immediately before a scan.

What it does not do: no persistence, no services, no drivers, no autorun, no
network traffic, no credential access, no code injection (no WriteProcessMemory,
no CreateRemoteThread, no hooks). The import table has no clipboard calls, no
key-state polling, no crypto-provider calls and no temp-directory writes;
SHA-256 is implemented inside the program, and clipboard copies use WM_COPY from
the program's own read-only edit control. No byte of another process is changed:
only VirtualQueryEx and ReadProcessMemory are used.

Build: x64, statically linked MinGW/UCRT, not packed, no UPX, no obfuscated or
encrypted strings, complete version resource (HYPERSENSEGLOBAL), asInvoker
manifest, Authenticode-signed. File: 1,852,920 bytes x64.
SHA-256: bcd48830528cb21166c4370c7a7e90b93e74ee12b0ede23d96e10fa15deb4df0
VirusTotal: engine counts change per build - check the report for the SHA-256 above.

Please reclassify this file and remove the Program:Win32/Wacapew.Cml detection.

Notes
-----
* Submit the exact hash above. Any rebuild changes it and you start over, so
  report the build you actually ship.
* Do not chase the verdict name with rebuilds. Microsoft's cloud ML re-scores
  the same bytes over time: this project's 745206FD... build went from
  2/70 (Wacapew) to 1/70 (clean) with no code change and no submission, and
  later builds came back as Wacatac.C!ml and then Wacapew.Cml again. A rebuild
  changes the hash, not the behaviour, so the result is a coin toss.
* This tool class is flagged by design: Process Hacker, System Informer, Cheat
  Engine and x64dbg are all routinely detected as PUA/hacktool until the vendor
  whitelists them. The submission above is the mechanism that does that.
* Microsoft normally answers a developer submission in 24-72 h and pushes an
  updated signature. Re-scan on VirusTotal after the definition update; the
  VirusTotal copy of the Microsoft engine lags the local one by some hours.
* If SmartScreen also blocks the download, the same submission covers it; a
  code-signing certificate (docs/FALSE_POSITIVES.md section 1) is the durable
  fix for reputation and lowers the ML weight of an unsigned binary.
* Do not "fix" this with packers, crypters or string obfuscation: that raises
  the ML score and turns a false positive into a real hacktool classification.

`docs/USER_GUIDE.md`:

```md
# HYPERSENSEGLOBAL™ — Live Usermode Memory Analysis Tool V1

Manual for the HYPERSENSEGLOBAL front end - see the [project README](../README.md) for the overview and downloads.

A dark, single-window front end for the [Moneta](https://github.com/forrest-orr/moneta)
usermode memory scanner. No command line, no flags to memorise: pick a target, flip the
toggles, press **RUN ANALYSIS**, read the live output, save the report.

```
ENGINE CREDITS : Forrest Orr & Gabriel Landau  (Moneta, GPLv3)
TOOL BUILD     : HYPERSENSEGLOBAL V1 — zero-crash build
DEVELOPED BY   : AG HYDRAXFF  (@hydraxff_yt)
INSTAGRAM      : https://www.instagram.com/hydraxff_yt/
```

---

## What it does

Moneta walks the virtual address space of a live process (or every process) looking for
memory that behaves like injected code: private executable regions, modules that do not
match their on-disk image, CLR JIT artefacts, unsigned modules, WOW64 transition stubs, and
so on. HYPERSENSEGLOBAL drives that engine and turns its console text into a readable,
colour-coded live feed plus a shareable report.

* Target a running process by name/PID, or scan **ALL PROCESSES**
* **SEARCH** box filters the target list live by process name, path or PID
* `SELECT .EXE` finds an executable on disk and auto-selects the matching running process
* **Moneta is compiled into the tool** — one standalone `HyperSense64.exe`, no external engine file
* Memory selection: **IOC**, **ALL**, **REGION**, **REFERENCED** (with address/size fields)
* Verbosity: **SURFACE**, **DETAIL**, **DEBUG**
* Options: permission statistics, scan-from-allocation-base, memory dump (`-d`)
* IOC filters: all filters, unsigned modules, metadata modules, CLR heap, CLR prvx, Wow64 init
* Live streaming output, 30 000-line viewer cap (the full output is always kept for the report)
* Moneta-style colouring: turquoise process metadata, cyan addresses, gold memory types,
  yellow descriptors (Heap/TEB/Stack/.NET) and **red IOC descriptions**
* Live IOC hit counter (viewer header, status bar) and a red `[ RESULT ]` summary when a scan ends
* **STOP** terminates a running scan instantly without freezing the window
* **SAVE TXT** / **SAVE HTML** write a report with host, SID, timestamps, flags, result and full output

---

## Requirements

* Windows 10 / 11, x64
* To build: MSYS2 with the **UCRT64** toolchain (`g++`), or any MinGW-w64 g++ 13+
* The Moneta engine sources (`../Source`, `../Headers`) are compiled directly into the
  executable, so **no `Moneta64.exe` is required** next to the tool.

The tool runs **asInvoker** — no administrator prompt unless you press **ELEVATE**.
Elevation is only needed to inspect protected / elevated processes.

---

## Build

```bat
cd HyperSense
build.bat
```

Output: `HyperSense\HyperSense64.exe`, automatically copied to the repository root as `HyperSense64.exe`.
The binary contains both the GUI and the Moneta engine — copy that single file anywhere and
it works.

The build script:

1. adds `C:\msys64\ucrt64\bin` to `PATH` (the compiler needs its own DLLs),
2. compiles `res\resource.rc` (icon + manifest + version info) with `windres`,
3. compiles `src\main.cpp` plus every `..\Source\*.cpp` engine translation unit,
4. statically links everything — no runtime dependencies beyond the Windows system DLLs,
5. regenerates `res\icon.ico` with `tools\make_icon.py` if the icon is missing.

> The repository path contains `!!`. `build.bat` therefore keeps cmd's *delayed expansion*
> disabled, otherwise cmd would strip those characters from every expanded path.

### Signing / antivirus

A memory scanner that reads other processes is a magnet for ML false positives. Sign the
final build and report the hash to the vendors that flag it - full procedure, submission
text and `HyperSense/tools/sign.ps1` usage in **[FALSE_POSITIVES.md](FALSE_POSITIVES.md)**.

---

## Usage

1. Start the target program you want to inspect.
2. Launch `HyperSense64.exe`.
3. Choose the target in **TARGET PROCESS**, or press **REFRESH** after starting a new program.
   Type in **SEARCH** to narrow the list by process name, full path or PID; the header shows
   how many entries match.
4. Pick the memory selection and verbosity, adjust the option/filter toggles.
5. **RUN ANALYSIS** — output streams live into the right-hand pane. IOC region lines are
   printed in red, memory types in gold and descriptors in yellow, mirroring Moneta's console.
6. **SAVE TXT** or **SAVE HTML** - each button opens a save dialog already set to that format.

When the engine finishes, the viewer adds a `[ RESULT ]` line: red with the number of flagged
regions when IOCs were found, green when the target is clean. The same verdict is written to
the report as the `RESULT` row.

### Built-in engine

There is nothing to locate or install. The Moneta engine is compiled into the executable and
runs as a hidden child of the tool itself:

```
HyperSense64.exe --engine -m ioc -p <PID> -v detail --option statistics --filter *
```

Running the engine in a child process keeps a scan failure away from the UI: even if the
engine hits a bug, the window stays responsive and reports the failure instead of crashing.

### Flags the GUI builds

| Control | Command line |
| --- | --- |
| Memory selection IOC / ALL / REGION / REFERENCED | `-m ioc` / `-m *` / `-m region` / `-m referenced` |
| Target | `-p <PID>` or `-p *` |
| Verbosity SURFACE / DETAIL / DEBUG | `-v surface` / `-v detail` / `-v debug` |
| Dump selected memory | `-d` |
| Permission statistics | `--option statistics` (comma-combined with `from-base`) |
| Scan from allocation base | `--option from-base` |
| All filters / individual filters | `--filter *` or `--filter unsigned-modules clr-heap ...` |
| REGION / REFERENCED | `--address 0x...` `--region-size 0x...` |

The exact command line used for a run is echoed into the viewer and stored in the report.

### Memory dump

With **Dump selected memory (-d)** enabled, output files land in `HYPERSENSE_DUMPS\` next to
the launcher, so the working folder stays clean.

---

## Report contents

```
HYPERSENSEGLOBAL(TM) — Live Usermode Memory Analysis Tool | V1

REPORT GENERATED : 2026-10-27 12:56:29.587  (UTC+05:30)
UTC TIME         : 2026-10-27 07:26:29.587 UTC
COMPUTER NAME    : MASTER-PC
ACCOUNT          : MASTER-PC\HIMAXKA
USER SID         : S-1-5-21-...-1001
OPERATING SYSTEM : Windows 10 Pro 22H2 (10.0.19045, x64)
ELEVATION        : ADMINISTRATOR
ENGINE           : Built-in - Moneta v1.0.1 (compiled into this tool)

TARGET / SCAN STARTED / SCAN FINISHED / SCAN DURATION
MEMORY SELECTION / VERBOSITY / OPTIONS / FILTERS / EXIT CODE
RESULT           : 11 suspicious regions flagged
COMMAND LINE     : ...

--- full engine output ---

Engine credits, tool build, developer, Instagram
```

The HTML variant is a self-contained dark-themed page (no external assets) suitable for
attaching to a ticket or a chat. It uses the same colour scheme as the live viewer, so IOC
descriptions stay red and memory types stay gold.

---

## Notes & limits

* **Moneta returns exit code 1 on success.** The exit code is reported as-is.
* A scan of *every* process at **DETAIL** verbosity produces a very large amount of text;
  the viewer stops rendering at 30 000 lines (and says so), while the report keeps everything
  up to 64 MB.
* The engine is a separate 64-bit console process. It cannot inspect 64-bit processes if you
  replace it with a 32-bit build.
* Region / Referenced modes require a valid address; the tool validates the field before
  launching the engine.
* Some protected processes are only accessible when the tool runs elevated.

---

## Project layout

```
HyperSense/
  src/main.cpp          entire application (UI, process list, runner, report writer)
  res/resource.rc       icon, manifest, version info
  res/app.manifest      asInvoker, common controls v6, DPI aware
  res/icon.ico          HYPERSENSEGLOBAL brand icon (multi-size)
  build.bat             UCRT64 build script
  tools/make_icon.py    fallback icon generator (only used if res/icon.ico is missing)
  tools/sign.ps1        Authenticode signing + verification helper
  tools/*.ps1           UI automation used to verify builds
```

`docs/WHATS_NEW.md`:

```md
# What's new — `v1.0.2` vs the last public build (`v1.0.1`)

The public `1.0.1` build was the sidebar GUI with a single live log. Everything below is what
this build adds or changes. The full item-by-item history is in
[`CHANGELOG.md`](../CHANGELOG.md).

| Area | `1.0.1` (old) | `1.0.2` (this build) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Log area | one live output pane | **`OUTPUT` / `COMMAND` / `DETAILS`** tabs; the log keeps streaming while another tab is in front |
| Help | none | **tooltip on every control** — buttons, modes, filters, fields, output, shortcuts |
| Command line | generated internally, not shown | **editable `COMMAND` box** with the exact `moneta64`-equivalent line; `Enter` parses and runs it, `Esc` restores it |
| Command presets | none | **`PRESET` dropdown**: `-m ioc -p <pid>`, `-v detail`, `--filter *`, `-d`, `-v detail --option statistics --filter *`, `-m * -p <pid> -v detail` |
| Classic mode | none | **`CLASSIC MODE` toggle** — one-row `moneta64.exe`-style screen: search, process list, PID override, presets, command, `RUN` / `STOP` / `SAVE` / `CLEAR` / `REFRESH` |
| Output menu | none | **right-click**: copy selection / line / all, open path, show in folder, **Search on VirusTotal**, save TXT/HTML, clear. `Ctrl+click` opens a path directly |
| Argument coverage | fixed set | **all Moneta arguments** — `-m`, `-p`, `-v`, `-d`, `--option`, `--filter`, `--address`, `--region-size` |
| Unsigned modules | same colour as everything | **`FOCUS UNSIGNED`** toggle; unsigned modules are emphasised by default in the live view and in saved reports |
| Discord | none | **always-on Rich Presence** — scan target, mode, suspicious count and a live timer, with GitHub / Discord buttons |
| Window | fixed 1360×900 | **fits the work area**; sidebar picks one of five layouts for the real height (100 % → 150 % scaling), even frame insets, minimum size enforced |
| First paint | sidebar could clip on short screens | layout chosen before the first frame; no stray control box, no clipped `IOC FILTERS` / footer |
| Single instance | every launch opened a new window | second launch focuses the existing window; one process |
| Save confirmation | plain message | popup with `OPEN FILE` / `OPEN FOLDER` / `CLOSE` and wrapped paths |
| Clipboard | Win32 clipboard API (`OpenClipboard` / `SetClipboardData`) | the log control's own `WM_COPY` |
| Ctrl+click | `GetKeyState` / `GetAsyncKeyState` polling | `MK_CONTROL` from the mouse message |
| SHA-256 (VirusTotal) | CryptoAPI (`CryptAcquireContext` / `CryptHashData`) | local FIPS 180-4 implementation, `src/sha256.h` |
| RPC debug log | `%TEMP%\hypersense_rpc.log` | `hypersense_rpc.log` next to the executable |
| File details | `1.0.0.0`, partial metadata | **`1.0.2`**, full Details tab: company, product, description, copyright, trademark, original filename, comments |
| Signature | none (unsigned) | **Authenticode-signed**, `CN=HYPERSENSEGLOBALT`, SHA-256, DigiCert RFC 3161 timestamp |
| Editions | one GUI exe | **GUI and CLASSIC** binaries, each shipped signed and unsigned — four downloads |
| SecureAge | `Malicious` | `Malicious` (the only flag on the last scanned build) |
| Microsoft | `2/70` flag (`Program:Win32/Wacapew.Cml`) | **clean — 1/71 on VirusTotal** on the last scanned build |

## Why the import table changed

A memory scanner that also imports the clipboard, key-state polling and a crypto provider reads
like an infostealer to cloud ML — that is what produced the `Wacatac`/`Wacapew` names. The three
features were re-implemented without those imports, with no functional loss:

* copy actions → `WM_COPY` on the program's own read-only log control;
* `Ctrl+click` → the `MK_CONTROL` flag already present in `WM_LBUTTONDOWN`;
* file hashing for the VirusTotal link → `src/sha256.h`.

## Verifying a build

```powershell
(Get-Item .\HyperSense64.exe).VersionInfo | Format-List CompanyName,ProductName,FileDescription,FileVersion,LegalCopyright,Comments
Get-FileHash .\HyperSense64.exe -Algorithm SHA256
Get-AuthenticodeSignature .\HyperSense64.exe | Format-List Status,@{n='SignedBy';e={$_.SignerCertificate.Subject}}
```

The four release files (`HyperSense/HXG SIGNED/`, version `1.0.2`):

| File | Size | Signature | SHA-256 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `HyperSense64 -unsigned.exe` | 1,845,760 | none | `568a1b428ddf4e46529d503a2dcd4b25fbaac708ed8312e0da426201d31b5f7a` |
| `HyperSense64 HXG SIGNED.exe` | 1,852,920 | `CN=HYPERSENSEGLOBALT` | `bcd48830528cb21166c4370c7a7e90b93e74ee12b0ede23d96e10fa15deb4df0` |
| `HyperSense64 CLASSIC -unsigned.exe` | 1,845,760 | none | `53d1e04483a9ec76baf2012e9d01c15904efe09be86458b6bc56d8f4b9a69542` |
| `HyperSense64 CLASSIC HXG SIGNED.exe` | 1,852,920 | `CN=HYPERSENSEGLOBALT` | `eb3764631f6d314f88271af0c039989307cc5da2ed9dc65e1a3cc7938f278a4a` |

`build.bat` builds the GUI flavour (opens in the sidebar layout), `build.bat classic` builds
`HyperSense64Classic.exe`, which opens in the `moneta64.exe`-style one-row layout
(`READY - CLASSIC` in the status bar). Everything else is identical; the `CLASSIC MODE` toggle
switches either build between the two layouts.

A rebuild changes the SHA-256 — and, with Microsoft's cloud ML, the verdict. Ship the build you
scanned; after signing, re-scan, because the hash is what the engines score.

```

`docs/upstream-moneta-readme.txt`:

```txt
   _____                        __          
  /     \   ____   ____   _____/  |______   
 /  \ /  \ /  _ \ /    \_/ __ \   __\__  \  
/    Y    (  <_> )   |  \  ___/|  |  / __ \_
\____|__  /\____/|___|  /\___  >__| (____  /
        \/            \/     \/          \/ 

Moneta v1.0 | Forrest Orr | 2020

REQUIRED

-m {*|region|referenced|ioc}
-p {*|PID}

OPTIONAL

-v {detail|debug|surface}
-d
--option {from-base|statistics}
--filter {unsigned-module|clr-prvx|clr-heap|metadata-modules}
--address <memory address>
--region-size <memory region size>


-m                  The memory to select and apply scanner settings to.

                    *                   Select all regions of committed memory.
                    ioc                 Select only regions which have suspicions associated with them.
                    region              Select only the region(s) which overlap with the region provided
                                        through the --address and --region-size arguments.
                    referenced          Select only regions which are referenced within the region(s)
                                        associated with the provided --address and --region-size arguments
-p                  The process(es) to scan. In the event that * is used, all accessible processes will
                    be enumerated and scanned.
--option            Additional actions to optionally apply to the memory selected from the scan.

                    from-base           All subregions associated with the allocation bases of all
                                        selected memory will also be selected.
                    statistics          Calculate permission statistics on the selected memory after a
                                        scan has completed.
-d                  Dump all selected memory to the local file system after each process scan is complete.
--address           A memory address in 0x* format to be used in conjunction with either the "region" or
                    "referenced" selection types.
--region-size       Optionally specify the size of the region of the provided "--address." The default is
                    a region size of 0.
-v                  The verbosity level with which to print information related to the selected memory.
                    The default is "surface"
--filter            The filters to apply when eliminating suspicions associated with selected memory.
                    
                    *                   Apply all filters. Only malware and unknown false positives shown.
                    unsigned-module     Regions of image memory associated with unsigned PE files.
                    metadata-modules    Regions of image memory stemming from signed Windows metadata PE
                                        files on disk.
                    clr-heap            Native executable heaps created during CLR initialization.
                    clr-prvx            Managed heaps associated with active CLR heaps and JIT code.
                    wow64-init          IOCs resulting from Wow64 process initialization such as certain
                                        modified system library code sections
										
EXAMPLES

Enumerate a detailed log of all committed memory in all processes on the OS:

    Moneta64.exe -m * -p * -v detail

Enumerate surface level information related to suspicious memory in a specific process:

    Moneta64.exe -m ioc -p 1234
	
Enumerate surface level information related to suspicious memory in a specific process from its allocation
base:

    Moneta64.exe -m ioc -p 1234 --option from-base

Dump a specific memory region by address within a specific process from its allocation base:

    Moneta64.exe -m region -p 1234 --option from-base --address 0x0000000077DD0000 -d
    
Enumerate surface level information related to suspicious memory in all processes and show memory
statistics on IOCs and region types when the scan is complete:

    Moneta64.exe -m ioc -p * --option statistics
	
Enumerate surface level information related to suspicious memory in all processes but exclude IOCs
stemming from unsigned modules and metadata modules:

    Moneta64.exe -m ioc -p * --filter unsigned-modules metadata-modules

```
